# ARCHITECTURE.md

# 💎 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Architecture

> Repository: Codex-Enterprise-IMPERIAL-Skills
> Visibility: 🔒 PRIVATE
> Project: IMPERIAL Core

---

# Architecture Mission

Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills (EIS) provide the governed professional capability layer for the entire IMPERIAL Core ecosystem.

Every skill is an architectural object rather than a prompt, script, or isolated function.

---

# Architecture Principle

Architecture Before Implementation

Every implementation must conform to the approved architecture.

Implementation never becomes architecture.

---

# Canonical Architecture

The following names are immutable.

- IMPERIAL Core
- Nano Core Agents
- HANTER
- Legion
- ORION
- CRYPTO IMPERIAL Ecosystem
- Capital Core
- Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills

---

# Multi-Orchestrator Architecture

Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills are designed for a federated architecture.

The system must support:

- multiple orchestrators;
- multiple organizations;
- multiple professional teams;
- multiple business domains;
- dynamic Nano Core Agent scaling.

A single unrestricted orchestrator is prohibited.

---

# Enterprise Skill

Each Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill is treated as an independent governed capability.

Every skill contains:

- identity;
- metadata;
- version;
- owner;
- permissions;
- policy;
- trust tier;
- execution profile;
- verification;
- audit evidence;
- lifecycle.

---

# Skill Lifecycle

Every skill passes through the following lifecycle.

Draft

↓

Engineering

↓

Verification

↓

Approval

↓

Production

↓

Maintenance

↓

Deprecation

↓

Archive

No production deployment may bypass this lifecycle.

---

# AI Passport

Every Nano Core Agent executing Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills must possess an AI Passport.

The AI Passport identifies:

- identity;
- organization;
- professional role;
- authorized skills;
- permissions;
- trust level;
- operational status.

---

# Trust Architecture

Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills operate through governed trust levels.

Trust is assigned only after successful verification.

Trust may be reduced or revoked.

Trust is never permanent.

---

# Verification

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill must successfully complete:

- structural verification;
- policy verification;
- security verification;
- functional verification;
- integration verification.

Verification evidence must be retained.

---

# Security

Every skill operates under:

- Zero Trust;
- least privilege;
- deny-by-default;
- explicit authorization;
- sandbox isolation;
- complete audit logging.

---

# Repository Architecture

Repository responsibilities include:

- architecture;
- governance;
- implementation;
- verification;
- automation;
- lifecycle management;
- standards;
- documentation.

---

# Public / Private Boundary

This repository belongs entirely to the PRIVATE engineering boundary.

Public repositories must never expose:

- internal architecture;
- engineering governance;
- security implementation;
- orchestration logic;
- private documentation.

---

# Long-Term Vision

Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills become the professional capability layer shared across IMPERIAL Core while preserving architectural consistency, engineering quality, governance, and security.

---

# SECURITY.md

# 🔒 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Security Policy

> Repository: Codex-Enterprise-IMPERIAL-Skills
> Visibility: 🔒 PRIVATE
> Project: IMPERIAL Core

---

# Mission

Security is a mandatory architectural requirement.

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill must be secure by design.

Security cannot be added after implementation.

---

# Security Principles

The repository follows:

- Zero Trust
- Least Privilege
- Deny By Default
- Explicit Authorization
- Complete Auditability
- Secure By Design
- Defense In Depth

---

# Zero Trust

Nothing is trusted automatically.

Every request must be:

- authenticated;
- authorized;
- validated;
- logged.

Trust must always be verified.

---

# Least Privilege

Every component receives only the permissions required for its assigned responsibility.

Additional permissions are prohibited unless explicitly approved.

---

# Deny By Default

Every action is denied unless explicitly authorized.

This applies to:

- APIs;
- Skills;
- Agents;
- Services;
- External integrations;
- Administrative operations.

---

# Authentication

Every identity must be authenticated before any operation.

Supported identities include:

- Human operators;
- Nano Core Agents;
- HANTER;
- Internal services;
- Approved automation.

---

# Authorization

Authentication alone never grants access.

Authorization determines:

- accessible resources;
- permitted operations;
- execution limits;
- security boundaries.

---

# AI Passport

Every Nano Core Agent must possess a valid AI Passport before executing Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills.

The AI Passport verifies:

- identity;
- organization;
- permissions;
- trust level;
- operational status.

Execution is denied if the passport is invalid.

---

# Guardian Core

Guardian Core operates as an independent security domain.

Responsibilities:

- policy enforcement;
- anomaly detection;
- execution blocking;
- emergency isolation;
- continuous monitoring.

---

# Approval Gateway

Approval Gateway protects privileged operations.

Approval is required for:

- production deployment;
- privilege escalation;
- trust changes;
- policy modifications;
- architectural changes;
- critical integrations.

---

# Audit Ledger

Every security event must be recorded.

Examples include:

- authentication;
- authorization;
- deployment;
- policy violations;
- verification;
- revocation;
- administrative actions.

Audit evidence must never be silently modified.

---

# Secrets

The repository must never contain:

- passwords;
- API keys;
- private keys;
- tokens;
- production credentials;
- confidential certificates.

Secrets must always remain outside Git.

---

# Repository Classification

Repository Classification:

🔒 PRIVATE

All repository contents are confidential engineering assets.

---

# Public / Private Boundary

Private repositories may contain:

- architecture;
- engineering documentation;
- governance;
- verification;
- security implementation.

Public repositories must never expose these materials without explicit approval.

---

# Security Verification

Before production deployment every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill must pass:

- structural review;
- security review;
- dependency review;
- permission review;
- policy validation;
- integration validation.

Deployment is prohibited if any mandatory verification fails.

---

# Incident Response

When a security event occurs:

1. Detect.
2. Isolate.
3. Investigate.
4. Record.
5. Recover.
6. Improve.

Every incident becomes engineering knowledge.

---

# Continuous Improvement

Security is continuously reviewed.

Every repository change should strengthen—not weaken—the overall security posture.

---

Author and Owner

Romaskevich Alexander Nikolaevich

# Governance Principles

The repository follows:

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Governance Before Automation
- Security Before Deployment
- Evidence Before Trust
- Explicit Approval
- Continuous Improvement

---

# Architectural Authority

The highest architectural authority is:

**Romaskevich Alexander Nikolaevich**

Founder • Owner • Chief Systems Architect

Only the Architect approves changes affecting:

- architecture;
- governance;
- security;
- trust model;
- repository boundaries;

# Governance Objects

Governance applies to:

- Skills;

- Automation;

Every improvement must strengthen quality without violating approved architecture.

# REGISTRY.md

# 💎 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Registry

> Repository: Codex-Enterprise-IMPERIAL-Skills
> Visibility: 🔒 PRIVATE
> Project: IMPERIAL Core

---

# Mission

The Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Registry is the authoritative catalog of every governed Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill.

No skill exists outside the Registry.

---

# Registry Objectives

The Registry provides:

- Skill Identification
- Ownership
- Version Control
- Trust Management
- Lifecycle Management
- Dependency Tracking
- Verification Status
- Audit References

---

# Registry Rules

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill must be registered before:

- engineering;
- testing;
- verification;
- production deployment.

Unregistered skills are prohibited.

---

# Skill Identity

Each Skill must have a globally unique identifier.

Example:

EIS-000001

The identifier never changes.

---

# Required Metadata

Every registered skill must contain:

- Skill ID
- Canonical Name
- Description
- Owner
- Responsible Team
- Repository
- Version
- Status
- Trust Tier
- Permissions
- Verification State
- Dependencies
- Compatible Agents
- AI Passport Requirements
- Approval Status
- Lifecycle Stage
- Audit Reference

---

# Skill Status

Every skill must be exactly one of:

- Draft
- Engineering
- Review
- Verification
- Approved
- Production
- Deprecated
- Revoked
- Archived

---

# Version Management

Each Skill must follow semantic versioning.

Examples:

1.0.0

1.1.0

2.0.0

Version history must remain permanent.

---

# Trust Registry

Every Skill must record:

- Current Trust Tier
- Trust History
- Verification Date
- Reviewer
- Approval Evidence

---

# Dependency Registry

Dependencies must be explicitly declared.

Hidden dependencies are prohibited.

Dependency changes require verification.

---

# Permission Registry

Each Skill must define:

- Required Permissions
- Restricted Permissions
- Forbidden Permissions

Permissions must follow Least Privilege.

---

# Verification Registry

Verification records include:

- Structural Validation
- Security Validation
- Policy Validation
- Functional Validation
- Integration Validation

Incomplete verification blocks deployment.

---

# Audit Registry

Registry changes must record:

- Author
- Timestamp
- Change Reason
- Previous Value
- New Value

History must never be deleted.

---

# Public / Private Boundary

Registry data belongs to the PRIVATE engineering boundary.

Public repositories may expose only explicitly approved metadata.

---

# Registry Integrity

The Registry is the single source of truth.

Repository files, manifests, policies, documentation and implementations must remain synchronized with the Registry.

---

Romaskevich Alexander Nikolaevich
# MANIFEST.md

# 💎 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Manifest Standard

> Repository: Codex-Enterprise-IMPERIAL-Skills
> Visibility: 🔒 PRIVATE
> Project: IMPERIAL Core

---

# Mission

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill must be described by a standardized manifest.

The manifest is the official machine-readable identity of the skill.

A skill without a manifest is not a valid Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill.

---

# Manifest Purpose

The manifest enables:

- Skill Identification
- Version Control
- Dependency Management
- Permission Management
- Trust Validation
- Runtime Configuration
- Verification
- Automation
- Registry Synchronization

---

# Mandatory Manifest Fields

Every manifest must contain:

- Skill ID
- Canonical Name
- Version
- Description
- Category
- Owner
- Responsible Team
- Repository
- Status
- Trust Tier
- AI Passport Requirements
- Dependencies
- Required Permissions
- Restricted Permissions
- Verification State
- Lifecycle Stage

---

# Optional Fields

A manifest may additionally contain:

- Tags
- Runtime Profile
- Compatible Models
- Memory Requirements
- CPU Requirements
- GPU Requirements
- Network Policy
- Sandbox Profile
- External Integrations
- Documentation References

---

# Manifest Integrity

Every manifest must be:

- valid;
- complete;
- deterministic;
- version controlled;
- synchronized with the Registry.

---

# Manifest Validation

Before deployment the manifest must pass:

- schema validation;
- metadata validation;
- dependency validation;
- permission validation;
- trust validation;
- repository validation.

---

# Manifest Versioning

Manifest versions follow Semantic Versioning.

Examples:

1.0.0

1.1.0

2.0.0

Older manifest versions remain archived.

---

# Registry Synchronization

Every manifest must remain synchronized with:

- Registry
- Documentation
- Verification
- Audit Ledger
- Repository Structure

No divergence is permitted.

---

# Security Requirements

A manifest must never contain:

- passwords;
- API keys;
- private tokens;
- secrets;
- confidential credentials.

Sensitive information belongs outside the manifest.

---

# Public / Private Boundary

Manifest files belong to the PRIVATE engineering repository.

Only explicitly approved public metadata may be published externally.

---

# Long-Term Vision

The Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Manifest becomes the universal engineering standard used by Codex for every governed Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill across the entire IMPERIAL Core ecosystem.

---

# VERIFICATION.md

# 💎 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Verification Framework

> Repository: Codex-Enterprise-IMPERIAL-Skills
> Visibility: 🔒 PRIVATE
> Project: IMPERIAL Core

---

# Mission

Verification guarantees that every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill satisfies the architectural, engineering, security, and governance requirements before entering production.

Verification is mandatory.

No Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill may bypass verification.

---

# Verification Principles

The framework follows:

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Security Before Deployment
- Evidence Before Trust
- Repeatable Validation
- Deterministic Results
- Complete Auditability

---

# Verification Gates

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill must successfully pass the following gates.

## Gate G1

Structural Verification

Validates:

- Repository Structure
- Documentation
- Manifest
- Metadata
- Version

---

## Gate G2

Security Verification

Validates:

- Permissions
- Policies
- Zero Trust Compliance
- Least Privilege
- Sandbox Configuration

---

## Gate G3

Engineering Verification

Validates:

- Implementation
- Code Quality
- Reliability
- Dependencies
- Error Handling

---

## Gate G4

Functional Verification

Validates:

- Expected Behaviour
- Inputs
- Outputs
- Integration
- Regression

---

## Gate G5

Approval Verification

Validates:

- Approval Gateway
- Repository Governance
- Audit Evidence
- Production Readiness

---

# Verification Results

Each verification produces one result.

PASS

WARNING

FAILED

BLOCKED

Only PASS allows production deployment.

---

# Verification Evidence

Every verification must record:

- Verification ID
- Skill ID
- Version
- Date
- Reviewer
- Result
- Evidence
- Notes

Evidence must remain permanently available.

---

# Reverification

Reverification is required after:

- implementation changes;
- dependency updates;
- security updates;
- permission changes;
- trust modifications;
- architecture updates.

---

# Failure Policy

If any mandatory verification fails:

- deployment stops;
- trust is not increased;
- approval is denied;
- issue is recorded;
- engineering continues after correction.

---

# Trust Relationship

Trust is earned through successful verification.

Trust cannot be self-assigned.

Trust may be reduced after failed verification.

---

# Continuous Verification

Verification continues during the entire lifecycle.

Production status does not eliminate future verification requirements.

---

# Public / Private Boundary

Verification evidence belongs to the PRIVATE engineering boundary.

Only explicitly approved summaries may appear in public repositories.

---

# Long-Term Vision

The Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Verification Framework becomes the global engineering quality standard for every governed Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill implemented by Codex throughout the IMPERIAL Core ecosystem.

---

Author and Owner

Romaskevich Alexander Nikolaevich
# TRUST.md

# 💎 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Trust Framework

> Repository: Codex-Enterprise-IMPERIAL-Skills
> Visibility: 🔒 PRIVATE
> Project: IMPERIAL Core

---

# Mission

The Trust Framework governs how Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills earn, maintain, reduce, suspend, and revoke trust throughout their lifecycle.

Trust is earned.

Trust is never assumed.

Trust is continuously verified.

---

# Trust Principles

The framework follows:

- Zero Trust
- Evidence Before Trust
- Least Privilege
- Continuous Verification
- Explicit Approval
- Complete Auditability

---

# Trust Objectives

The Trust Framework ensures:

- Secure Skill Execution
- Controlled Capability Growth
- Transparent Decision Making
- Continuous Risk Assessment
- Engineering Integrity
- Repository Consistency

---

# Trust Tiers

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill belongs to one Trust Tier.

## T0

Blocked

Execution prohibited.

---

## T1

Experimental

Engineering environment only.

No production execution.

---

## T2

Verified

Internal execution allowed.

Restricted permissions.

---

## T3

Production

Approved for operational deployment.

Continuous monitoring required.

---

## T4

Critical

Mission-critical capability.

Highest verification requirements.

Continuous supervision.

---

# Trust Requirements

Trust is determined using:

- Architecture Compliance
- Verification Results
- Security Review
- Approval Status
- Audit Evidence
- Operational History

No single factor is sufficient.

---

# Trust Increase

Trust may increase only after:

- successful verification;
- successful testing;
- governance approval;
- repository validation;
- audit confirmation.

---

# Trust Reduction

Trust may be reduced when:

- verification fails;
- vulnerabilities appear;
- policy violations occur;
- unexpected behaviour is detected;
- engineering quality decreases.

---

# Trust Suspension

Immediate suspension occurs when:

- severe security issues appear;
- unauthorized modifications occur;
- identity cannot be verified;
- integrity cannot be guaranteed.

Suspended Skills cannot execute.

---

# Trust Revocation

Trust is permanently revoked when:

- malicious behaviour is confirmed;
- architectural integrity is lost;
- governance is violated;
- security requirements cannot be restored.

Revoked Skills remain archived for audit.

---

# Continuous Trust

Trust is continuously evaluated.

Production deployment never guarantees permanent trust.

Every execution may influence trust.

---

# AI Passport Integration

Trust evaluation includes AI Passport validation.

The AI Passport confirms:

- identity;
- organization;
- permissions;
- operational state;
- authorized capabilities.

Invalid AI Passports block execution.

---

# Repository Trust

Repository trust depends upon:

- documentation quality;
- implementation quality;
- verification completeness;
- security posture;
- governance compliance.

---

# LIFECYCLE.md

# 💎 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Lifecycle Framework

> Repository: Codex-Enterprise-IMPERIAL-Skills
> Visibility: 🔒 PRIVATE
> Project: IMPERIAL Core

---

# Mission

The Lifecycle Framework governs every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill from its creation to permanent archival.

Every Skill follows the same controlled lifecycle.

No Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill may bypass lifecycle governance.

---

# Lifecycle Principles

The framework follows:

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Governance Before Deployment
- Verification Before Trust
- Continuous Improvement
- Complete Traceability
- Immutable History

---

# Lifecycle Stages

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill progresses through the following stages.

## Stage 01

Draft

Initial architectural proposal.

No implementation permitted.

---

## Stage 02

Architecture

Architecture is approved.

Requirements are defined.

Interfaces are documented.

---

## Stage 03

Engineering

Implementation begins.

Repository artifacts are created.

No production execution.

---

## Stage 04

Verification

Mandatory validation begins.

Security.

Engineering.

Functional.

Policy.

Repository.

---

## Stage 05

Approval

Approval Gateway evaluates readiness.

Architectural compliance is confirmed.

Governance is verified.

---

## Stage 06

Production

Skill becomes operational.

Continuous monitoring begins.

Trust evaluation continues.

---

## Stage 07

Maintenance

Bug fixes.

Optimization.

Documentation updates.

Compatibility improvements.

---

## Stage 08

Deprecation

Skill is scheduled for retirement.

Migration guidance is prepared.

Production usage decreases.

---

## Stage 09

Archive

Skill becomes read-only.

Historical evidence remains permanently available.

Execution is prohibited.

---

# Lifecycle Metadata

Every lifecycle transition records:

- Skill ID
- Version
- Previous Stage
- New Stage
- Date
- Responsible Team
- Reviewer
- Approval Reference
- Audit Reference

---

# Transition Rules

No lifecycle transition is automatic.

Every transition requires:

- verification;
- governance review;
- audit recording;
- repository synchronization.

---

# Rollback

Rollback is permitted only through approved governance procedures.

Rollback must preserve:

- repository integrity;
- audit history;
- version history;
- architectural consistency.

---

# Continuous Monitoring

Production Skills remain continuously monitored for:

- reliability;
- security;
- policy compliance;
- trust changes;
- operational quality.

Monitoring never stops after deployment.

---

# Lifecycle Integrity

Every Skill must exist in exactly one lifecycle stage.

Duplicate lifecycle states are prohibited.

Undefined lifecycle states are prohibited.

---

# Public / Private Boundary

Lifecycle records belong to the PRIVATE engineering repository.

Only explicitly approved lifecycle summaries may appear in public repositories.

---

# Long-Term Vision

The Lifecycle Framework establishes a governed engineering process that ensures Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills remain secure, traceable, maintainable, and professionally managed throughout the entire IMPERIAL Core ecosystem.

---

Author and Owner

Romaskevich Alexander Nikolaevich
# PERMISSIONS.md

# 💎 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Permission Framework

> Repository: Codex-Enterprise-IMPERIAL-Skills
> Visibility: 🔒 PRIVATE
> Project: IMPERIAL Core

---

# Mission

The Permission Framework governs how Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills request, receive, use, monitor, and revoke permissions.

Permissions are granted—not assumed.

Every permission must have an explicit purpose.

---

# Permission Principles

The framework follows:

- Zero Trust
- Least Privilege
- Deny By Default
- Explicit Authorization
- Complete Auditability
- Continuous Verification

---

# Permission Categories

Every permission belongs to one category.

- Read
- Write
- Execute
- Network
- Storage
- System
- Administration
- External Integration

---

# Permission Scope

Every permission must define:

- Resource
- Operation
- Scope
- Duration
- Owner
- Approval Reference

Undefined permissions are prohibited.

---

# Required Permissions

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill must explicitly declare:

- Required Permissions
- Optional Permissions
- Restricted Permissions
- Forbidden Permissions

Hidden permissions are prohibited.

---

# Least Privilege

Every Skill receives only the permissions required for its approved purpose.

Unused permissions must be removed.

Permission expansion requires approval.

---

# Deny By Default

All operations are denied unless explicitly authorized.

This applies to:

- APIs
- Files
- Services
- Databases
- Models
- External Systems
- Internal Infrastructure

---

# Permission Approval

Permission changes require:

- architectural review;
- governance review;
- Approval Gateway authorization;
- audit recording.

---

# Permission Verification

Permissions must be verified before:

- engineering;
- testing;
- production deployment;
- trust elevation.

Verification failure blocks execution.

---

# Runtime Monitoring

Permission usage is continuously monitored.

The system records:

- granted permissions;
- denied permissions;
- permission violations;
- unexpected access;
- privilege escalation attempts.

---

# Permission Revocation

Permissions may be revoked when:

- no longer required;
- policy changes;
- security incidents;
- trust reduction;
- lifecycle transition;
- governance decision.

Revocation takes effect immediately.

---

# AI Passport Integration

Permission evaluation includes AI Passport validation.

The AI Passport determines:

- identity;
- organization;
- role;
- capability scope;
- authorized permissions.

Execution without a valid AI Passport is prohibited.

---

# Public / Private Boundary

Permission policies belong exclusively to the PRIVATE engineering repository.

Public repositories must never expose internal permission models.

---

# Long-Term Vision

The Permission Framework establishes a universal authorization model for every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill across the IMPERIAL Core ecosystem, ensuring secure, governed, and traceable capability execution.

---

Author and Owner

Romaskevich Alexander Nikolaevich

# Mission

Policies establish consistent behaviour, engineering discipline, governance, security, and compliance across the entire repository.

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill must comply with every applicable policy.

---

# Policy Principles

The framework follows:

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Security By Design
- Zero Trust
- Least Privilege
- Deny By Default
- Explicit Authorization
- Continuous Verification
- Evidence Before Trust

---

# Policy Categories

Policies are organized into the following domains.

- Architecture
- Governance
- Security
- Engineering
- Verification
- Permissions
- Trust
- Lifecycle
- Audit
- Compliance

---

# Architecture Policy

Every implementation must preserve the approved architecture.

Codex must never:

- redefine architecture;
- rename canonical components;
- bypass architectural governance;
- silently introduce incompatible changes.

---

# Security Policy

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill must:

- authenticate;
- authorize;
- validate;
- audit;
- isolate;
- protect confidential information.

Security is mandatory.

---

# Engineering Policy

Engineering standards require:

- readable implementation;
- modular design;
- documentation;
- deterministic behaviour;
- maintainability;
- traceable commits.

---

# Verification Policy

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill requires:

- structural verification;
- engineering verification;
- security verification;
- functional verification;
- governance verification.

Incomplete verification blocks production deployment.

---

# Permission Policy

Permissions must:

- follow Least Privilege;
- be explicitly declared;
- remain continuously monitored;
- be revocable;
- remain auditable.

Hidden permissions are prohibited.

---

# Trust Policy

Trust:

- is earned;
- is verified;
- is monitored;
- may be reduced;
- may be revoked.

Trust is never permanent.

---

# Lifecycle Policy

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill follows the approved lifecycle.

No lifecycle stage may be skipped.

Every transition requires governance and audit.

---

# Audit Policy

Every significant operation must generate audit evidence.

Audit records include:

- approvals;
- verification;
- deployments;
- trust changes;
- policy changes;
- lifecycle transitions.

Audit history must remain immutable.

---

# Compliance Policy

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill must comply with:

- repository standards;
- governance standards;
- security standards;
- engineering standards;
- architectural standards.

Compliance is continuously evaluated.

---

# Repository Policy

This repository is classified as:

🔒 PRIVATE

All engineering artifacts remain inside the private repository boundary.

Public publication requires explicit approval.

---

# Continuous Improvement

Policies evolve through governed architectural decisions.

Improvements must strengthen:

- engineering quality;
- repository consistency;
- operational safety;
- maintainability.

Architectural integrity must always be preserved.

Undeclared dependencies are prohibited.

---

# Version Compatibility

Every dependency specifies:

- Name
- Version
- Compatibility Range
- Minimum Version
- Maximum Version

Version ambiguity is prohibited.

---

# Dependency Validation

Every dependency must pass:

- Availability Validation
- Compatibility Validation
- Security Validation
- Repository Validation
- Trust Validation

Validation failure blocks deployment.

---

# Dependency Integrity

Every dependency must be:

- documented;
- version controlled;
- traceable;
- reproducible;
- verifiable.

---

# Dependency Updates

Dependency updates require:

- compatibility verification;
- engineering review;
- security review;
- governance approval;
- audit recording.

---

# Dependency Conflicts

Conflicting dependencies must be resolved before:

- testing;
- verification;
- production deployment.

Production deployment with unresolved dependency conflicts is prohibited.

---

# Runtime Dependencies

Runtime dependencies include:

- execution environment;
- policies;
- manifests;
- AI Passport validation;
- permission framework;
- monitoring services.

---

# Audit

Every dependency change records:

- Dependency ID
- Skill ID
- Previous Version
- New Version
- Reviewer
- Timestamp
- Audit Reference

---

# Public / Private Boundary

Dependency definitions belong exclusively to the PRIVATE engineering repository.

Public repositories must never expose internal dependency architecture.

---

# Long-Term Vision

The Dependency Framework establishes a universal dependency management standard for Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills, ensuring predictable integration, engineering consistency, security, and long-term maintainability throughout the IMPERIAL Core ecosystem.

# SKILL_VERSIONING.md

# 💎 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Versioning Framework

> Repository: Codex-Enterprise-IMPERIAL-Skills
> Visibility: 🔒 PRIVATE
> Project: IMPERIAL Core

---

# Mission

The Versioning Framework defines how Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills evolve without breaking architectural consistency, repository integrity, or production stability.

Every version must be traceable.

Every version must remain reproducible.

---

# Versioning Principles

The framework follows:

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Immutable History
- Full Traceability
- Backward Compatibility
- Deterministic Releases
- Continuous Verification

---

# Objectives

The Versioning Framework provides:

- Stable Engineering
- Controlled Evolution
- Safe Upgrades
- Repository Consistency
- Compatibility Management
- Historical Integrity

---

# Semantic Versioning

Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills use Semantic Versioning.

MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH

Example:

1.0.0

1.2.0

1.2.4

2.0.0

---

# Major Version

Increase MAJOR when:

- architecture changes;
- incompatible interfaces appear;
- breaking changes are introduced.

Major releases require architectural approval.

---

# Minor Version

Increase MINOR when:

- capabilities expand;
- backward compatibility remains;
- engineering improvements are added.

---

# Patch Version

Increase PATCH when:

- bugs are fixed;
- documentation improves;
- security patches are applied;
- implementation quality increases.

No architectural changes occur.

---

# Version Metadata

Every version records:

- Version
- Release Date
- Repository Revision
- Author
- Reviewer
- Approval Reference
- Verification Result
- Audit Reference

---

# Compatibility

Each version specifies:

- Supported Runtime
- Supported Manifest Version
- Supported Policy Version
- Supported Permission Version
- Supported Registry Version

Compatibility must be explicit.

---

# Upgrade Rules

Before upgrading:

- verify compatibility;
- verify dependencies;
- verify policies;
- verify permissions;
- verify trust;
- verify repository consistency.

---

# Rollback

Rollback is permitted only when:

- verification fails;
- production instability appears;
- security risk exists;
- governance authorizes rollback.

Rollback preserves audit history.

---

# Release Management

Every release requires:

- verification;
- approval;
- documentation update;
- repository synchronization;
- audit recording.

---

# Version History

Version history is permanent.

Historical versions remain available for:

- audit;
- engineering review;
- compatibility analysis;
- repository reconstruction.

Deletion is prohibited.

---

# Repository Classification

Repository Classification:

🔒 PRIVATE

Version history belongs exclusively to the private engineering repository.

---

# Long-Term Vision

The Versioning Framework establishes a universal engineering version control standard for Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills, ensuring predictable evolution, architectural consistency, repository integrity, and long-term maintainability across the IMPERIAL Core ecosystem.

---

Author and Owner

Romaskevich Alexander Nikolaevich

# Mission

Routing guarantees that the correct Skill is assigned to the correct AI Agent at the correct time.

Routing must always remain deterministic, governed, secure, and auditable.

---

# Routing Principles

The framework follows:

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Multi-Orchestrator Architecture
- Zero Trust
- Explicit Authorization
- Least Privilege
- Continuous Verification
- Evidence Before Trust

---

# Routing Objectives

The Routing Framework provides:

- Intelligent Skill Selection
- Capability Matching
- Load Distribution
- Runtime Optimization
- Trust-Based Execution
- Continuous Monitoring

# Routing Inputs

- AI Passport
- Agent Role

- Skill Availability
- Lifecycle State
- Current Load
- Policy Restrictions

# Routing Decision

- Selected Skill

- Permission Validation

# Routing Priority

Priority evaluation includes:

Critical

High

Normal

Low

Background

- Skill Registration
- Manifest
- Permissions
- AI Passport
- Trust Tier
- Lifecycle
- Runtime Compatibility

Validation failure blocks execution.

---

# Mission

The Execution Framework governs how Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills are initialized, authorized, executed, monitored, completed, and terminated.

Every execution must remain deterministic, secure, governed, and fully auditable.

Execution never bypasses architecture.

---

# Execution Principles

The framework follows:

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Multi-Orchestrator Architecture
- Zero Trust
- Least Privilege
- Explicit Authorization
- Continuous Verification
- Evidence Before Trust

---

# Execution Objectives

The framework provides:

- Safe Execution
- Deterministic Behaviour
- Runtime Governance
- Continuous Monitoring
- Auditability
- Failure Isolation

---

# Execution Pipeline

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill follows the same execution pipeline.

Discovery

↓

Selection

↓

Authentication

↓

Authorization

↓

AI Passport Validation

↓

Permission Validation

↓

Dependency Validation

↓

Runtime Allocation

↓

Execution

↓

Monitoring

↓

Completion

↓

Audit Recording

---

# Execution Requirements

Before execution begins the system validates:

- Skill Registration
- Manifest
- Repository Integrity
- Verification Status
- Trust Tier
- Lifecycle Stage
- Runtime Compatibility
- Approval Status

Execution is denied if any mandatory validation fails.

---

# Runtime Allocation

Execution selects an approved runtime based upon:

- CPU Capacity
- GPU Capacity
- Memory
- Latency
- Availability
- Security Policies
- Sandbox Profile

---

# Execution Monitoring

During execution the framework continuously monitors:

- Runtime Health
- Memory Usage
- CPU Usage
- GPU Usage
- Policy Compliance
- Permission Usage
- Security Events
- Trust State

---

# Execution Constraints

Execution must never:

- bypass Approval Gateway;
- bypass AI Passport validation;
- bypass permission checks;
- execute revoked Skills;
- execute deprecated Skills;
- execute outside approved runtime.

---

# Failure Handling

If execution fails:

- stop execution;
- isolate runtime;
- preserve evidence;
- notify monitoring;
- record audit;
- prevent undefined behaviour.

Automatic recovery requires governance approval.

---

# Completion

Successful execution records:

- Skill ID
- Version
- Runtime
- AI Passport
- Execution Duration
- Result
- Audit Reference

Completion does not increase trust automatically.

---

# Audit

Every execution generates immutable audit evidence.

Audit records include:

- Execution ID

- Agent
- Runtime
- Result
- Verification State
- Trust Tier

🔒 PRIVATE

# Mission

---

# Sandbox Principles

The framework follows:

- Zero Trust
- Least Privilege
- Deny By Default
- Runtime Isolation
- Explicit Authorization
- Continuous Monitoring
- Complete Auditability

---

# Objectives

The Sandbox Framework provides:

- Runtime Isolation
- Resource Protection
- Controlled Execution
- Secure Interaction
- Failure Containment

---

# Sandbox Components

Every sandbox contains:

- Runtime Environment
- Permission Controller
- Resource Manager
- Network Policy
- File System Policy
- Monitoring Agent

---

# Isolation Rules

Every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill executes inside an isolated environment.

- Operating System
- Repository
- Runtime
- Memory
- Network
- Storage

Cross-Sandbox access is prohibited.

---

# Resource Limits

Each sandbox defines:

- CPU Limit
- GPU Limit
- Memory Limit
- Storage Limit
- Network Limit

Unlimited resources are prohibited.

# Network Policy

Every network connection requires:

- explicit authorization;
- policy validation;
- audit recording.

Unauthorized outbound communication is prohibited.

---

# Runtime Monitoring

- CPU
- GPU
- Memory
- Storage
- Network
- Runtime Health
- Policy Compliance
- Security Events

# Sandbox Failure

If sandbox integrity is compromised:

- stop execution;
- isolate environment;
- preserve evidence;
- notify monitoring;
- record audit;
- prevent further execution.

# AI Passport Integration

Sandbox execution validates:

- AI Passport
- Trust Tier

- Organization

Execution is denied when validation fails.

# Audit

# Repository Classification

Repository Classification:

🔒 PRIVATE

- Stable Integration
- Predictable Behaviour
- Safe Upgrades
- Version Consistency

Compatibility is evaluated for:

- AI Passports
- Skill Manifests
- Registries
- Policies
- Permissions
- Runtime
- Verification
- Audit
- Repository Structure

---

# Version Compatibility

Every Skill explicitly declares:

- Minimum Supported Version
- Recommended Version
- Maximum Supported Version
- Deprecated Versions

Version ambiguity is prohibited.

---

# Runtime Compatibility

Every Skill specifies compatibility with:

- Runtime Version
- Operating Environment
- Sandbox Version
- Execution Profile
- Resource Requirements

Unsupported runtimes are prohibited.

---

Every Skill defines compatible execution for:

- HANTER
- Nano Core Agents
- Legion
- ORION
- Approved Internal Services

Unsupported agents may not execute the Skill.

---

# Dependency Compatibility

Compatibility validates:

- Internal Dependencies
- External Libraries
- Runtime Components
- Shared Policies
- Verification Modules

All dependency versions must remain compatible.

---

# Manifest Compatibility

Every Skill Manifest must remain synchronized with:

- Registry
- Version
- Lifecycle
- Trust Tier
- Verification Status

Manifest inconsistency blocks deployment.

---

# Compatibility Verification

Compatibility verification includes:

- Version Validation
- Dependency Validation
- Runtime Validation
- Manifest Validation
- Policy Validation
- Repository Validation

Verification failure blocks production.

---

# Continuous Compatibility

Compatibility is continuously evaluated after:

- repository updates;
- runtime upgrades;
- dependency changes;
- policy modifications;
- architecture revisions.

---

# Mission

# Architecture Principles

# Objectives

- Relationship Mapping
- Dependency Analysis
- Circular Dependency Detection

- Upgrade Planning
- Repository Intelligence
- Runtime Optimization

# Graph Nodes

Every node represents one governed object.

Supported node types include:

- Enterprise Skill
- Manifest
- Policy
- Permission
- Runtime
- AI Passport
- Verification
- Registry Entry
- Audit Record

---

# Graph Edges

Edges represent approved relationships.

Supported relationships include:

- Depends On
- Requires
- Uses
- Validates
- Authorizes
- Executes
- Monitors
- Produces
- Consumes
- References

---

# Dependency Rules

Every dependency must be:

- declared;
- versioned;
- verified;
- documented;
- auditable.

Implicit dependencies are prohibited.

---

# Circular Dependency Detection

The framework continuously detects:

- direct cycles;
- indirect cycles;
- recursive chains;
- invalid dependency loops.

Circular production dependencies are prohibited.

---

# Impact Analysis

Before any repository change the graph evaluates:

- affected Skills;
- affected Policies;
- affected Permissions;
- affected Runtime;
- affected Verification;
- affected Documentation.

Every engineering change must include dependency impact analysis.

---

# Graph Validation

Validation verifies:

- node integrity;
- edge integrity;
- version compatibility;
- repository synchronization;
- governance consistency.

Validation failure blocks production deployment.

---

# Repository Synchronization

The Dependency Graph remains synchronized with:

- Registry
- Manifest
- Catalog
- Verification
- Lifecycle
- Trust Framework
- Runtime Framework

Synchronization is mandatory.

---

# Audit

- Graph Change ID
- Timestamp
- Modified Nodes
- Modified Edges
- Reviewer
- Audit Reference

History remains immutable.

---

# Repository Classification

Repository Classification:

🔒 PRIVATE

Dependency Graph architecture belongs exclusively to the private engineering repository.

---

# Long-Term Vision

# 💎 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Composition Framework

> Project: IMPERIAL Core

---

# Mission

The Composition Framework defines how multiple Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills are combined into governed, reusable, scalable, and deterministic capability pipelines.

A Composition is not a new Skill.

It is an approved orchestration of existing governed Skills.

---

# Architecture Principles

The framework follows:

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Multi-Orchestrator Architecture
- Zero Trust
- Explicit Dependencies
- Explicit Authorization
- Continuous Verification
- Complete Auditability

---

# Objectives

The Composition Framework provides:

- Skill Reusability
- Capability Orchestration
- Workflow Standardization
- Engineering Consistency
- Modular Execution
- Enterprise Scalability

---

# Composition Definition

A Composition contains:

- Composition ID
- Canonical Name
- Description
- Owner
- Version
- Status
- Lifecycle Stage
- Trust Tier

Every Composition must be registered.

---

# Composition Components

A Composition may include:

- Enterprise Skills
- Runtime Policies
- Verification Rules
- Permission Policies
- Approval Rules
- Monitoring Policies
- Audit Requirements

No unregistered component may participate.

---

# Execution Order

Every Composition defines:

- Execution Sequence
- Parallel Execution
- Conditional Execution
- Retry Rules
- Failure Rules
- Completion Rules

Execution order must remain deterministic.

---

# Composition Validation

Before execution the framework validates:

- Skill Registration
- Dependency Graph
- Manifest Compatibility
- AI Passport
- Permission Model
- Runtime Compatibility
- Trust Tier
- Verification Status

Validation failure blocks execution.

---

# Failure Handling

When a Composition fails:

- stop affected execution;
- preserve evidence;
- isolate failed component;
- notify monitoring;
- record audit;
- evaluate rollback.

Undefined recovery is prohibited.

---

# Composition Audit

Every Composition execution records:

- Composition ID
- Version
- Skills Used
- Runtime
- AI Passport
- Execution Result
- Timestamp
- Audit Reference

Audit evidence remains immutable.

---

# Repository Synchronization

Every Composition remains synchronized with:

- Registry
- Catalog
- Manifest
- Dependency Graph
- Runtime Framework
- Verification Framework
- Audit Framework

Repository inconsistency is prohibited.

---

# Public / Private Boundary

Composition architecture belongs exclusively to the PRIVATE engineering repository.

Internal orchestration logic must never be exposed publicly.

---

# Long-Term Vision

The Composition Framework establishes the universal orchestration model for Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills, enabling Codex, HANTER, Nano Core Agents, Legion, ORION, and future IMPERIAL Core systems to assemble complex professional capabilities from governed reusable Skills while preserving architectural consistency, security, and enterprise scalability.

# Architecture Principles

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Disaster Recovery By Design

- Zero Trust
- Controlled Recovery
- Continuous Verification

- Disaster Detection
- Recovery Planning
- Recovery Automation
- Business Continuity
- Infrastructure Restoration
- Enterprise Recovery Governance

---

# Disaster Categories

Supported disaster categories include:

- Infrastructure Failure
- Data Center Failure
- Regional Failure
- Cloud Provider Failure
- Storage Failure
- Network Failure
- Security Incident
- Enterprise Service Failure
- Multi-System Failure

---

# Recovery Sources

Recovery events may originate only from:

- Monitoring Framework
- Resilience Engine
- High Availability Engine
- Runtime Engine
- Security Framework
- Approved Infrastructure Providers

Unauthorized recovery events are prohibited.

---

# Disaster Recovery Pipeline

↓

Identity Validation

↓

↓

Recovery Plan Selection

↓

Infrastructure Restoration

↓

Runtime Recovery

↓

Data Verification

↓

Operational Validation

↓

Audit Recording

---

# Recovery Strategies

Supported recovery strategies include:

- Cold Recovery
- Warm Recovery
- Hot Recovery
- Multi-Region Recovery
- Cross-Cloud Recovery
- Point-in-Time Recovery
- Immutable Backup Recovery
- Enterprise Continuity Recovery

Recovery strategy must be explicitly approved.

---

# Recovery Validation

Before restoration the framework validates:

- Repository Integrity
- Backup Integrity

- Trust Tier
- Policy Compliance
- Infrastructure Readiness
- Recovery Readiness

Validation failure blocks restoration.

---

# Recovery Objectives

Every recovery plan defines:

- Recovery Time Objective (RTO)
- Recovery Point Objective (RPO)

- Recovery Scope

- Success Conditions

Objectives are continuously monitored.

---

# Continuous Readiness

The framework continuously evaluates:

- Backup Availability
- Recovery Readiness
- Infrastructure Health
- Recovery Testing
- Recovery Success Rate
- Business Continuity Status

Continuous readiness does not imply disaster activation.

# Failure Handling

When recovery validation fails:

- suspend restoration;

- require executive engineering review.

# 💎 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Backup Engine Framework

---

# Mission

The Backup Engine Framework governs how Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills create, verify, encrypt, replicate, retain, restore, and audit enterprise backups.

Every backup must be recoverable, encrypted, verified, governed, and continuously auditable.

---

# Architecture Principles

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Backup Before Recovery
- Multi-Orchestrator Architecture
- Zero Trust
- Encryption By Default
- Immutable Evidence
- Continuous Verification
- Complete Auditability

---

# Objectives

The Backup Engine provides:

- Backup Creation
- Backup Verification
- Backup Encryption
- Backup Replication
- Backup Retention
- Restore Management
- Recovery Readiness
- Enterprise Governance

---

# Protected Assets

The framework protects:

- Enterprise Skills
- Skill Registry
- Skill Catalog
- Runtime Configuration
- Knowledge Base
- Memory
- State
- Documentation
- Source Code
- Audit Ledger
- Deployment Artifacts

---

# Backup Lifecycle

Every backup follows:

Request

↓

Validation

↓

Encryption

↓

Backup Creation

↓

Integrity Verification

↓

Replication

↓

Retention Registration

↓

Monitoring

↓

Restore Testing

↓

Audit Recording

---

# Backup Validation

Before creation the framework validates:

- Repository Integrity
- AI Passport
- Trust Tier
- Policy Compliance
- Source Integrity
- Storage Availability
- Encryption Availability

Validation failure blocks backup creation.

---

# Backup Strategies

Supported strategies include:

- Full Backup
- Incremental Backup
- Differential Backup
- Snapshot Backup
- Immutable Backup
- Point-in-Time Backup
- Cross-Region Backup
- Air-Gapped Backup

---

# Encryption

Every backup requires:

- Encryption At Rest
- Encryption In Transit
- KMS or HSM Integration
- Key Rotation
- Digital Signature
- Integrity Hash

Plaintext backups are prohibited.

---

# Restore Validation

Before restoration the framework verifies:

- Backup Integrity
- Signature
- Hash
- Runtime Compatibility
- Manifest Compatibility
- Registry Consistency
- Policy Compliance

Only verified backups may be restored.

---

# Monitoring

Continuous monitoring evaluates:

- Backup Success
- Replication Status
- Encryption Status
- Storage Capacity
- Verification Status
- Restore Readiness

---

# Failure Handling

When backup validation fails:

- reject backup;
- preserve evidence;
- notify monitoring;
- create audit record;
- require engineering review.

---

# Backup Audit

Every operation records:

- Backup ID
- Source
- Version
- Operation
- Timestamp
- Result
- Audit Reference

Audit history remains immutable.

---

# Guardian Core

Guardian Core may:

- block unsafe backups;
- detect integrity violations;
- isolate compromised backups;
- trigger emergency review.

---

# Approval Gateway

Approval Gateway is required for:

- Production Restore
- Critical Restore
- Immutable Backup Removal
- Retention Policy Changes

---

# Repository Synchronization

The Backup Engine remains synchronized with:

- Storage Engine
- Runtime Engine
- Data Engine
- Verification Framework
- Trust Framework
- Guardian Core
- Approval Gateway
- Audit Ledger

---

# Repository Classification

Repository Classification:

🔒 PRIVATE

Backup Engine architecture belongs exclusively to the private engineering repository.

---

# Long-Term Vision

The Backup Engine Framework establishes the enterprise backup architecture for Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills, enabling Codex, HANTER, Nano Core Agents, Legion, ORION, and future IMPERIAL Core systems to securely preserve, restore, and govern enterprise assets through verified, encrypted, and continuously audited backup procedures.

---

# SDK_ARCHITECTURE.md

# 💎 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills SDK Architecture

> Repository: Codex-Enterprise-IMPERIAL-Skills
> Visibility: 🔒 PRIVATE
> Project: IMPERIAL Core
> System: Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills (EIS)
> Status: Codex-Ready Engineering Specification

---

# Mission

The Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills SDK provides the governed engineering foundation required to create, validate, test, package, register, deploy, execute, monitor, and retire Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills.

The SDK transforms approved architecture into reusable implementation contracts.

Codex must use this SDK architecture as the foundation for future production code.

---

# Architecture Principles

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Contract-First Engineering
- Multi-Orchestrator Architecture
- Zero Trust
- Deny By Default
- Least Privilege
- Evidence Before Trust
- Secure By Design
- Test Before Deployment
- Complete Auditability

---

# SDK Objectives

The SDK must provide:

- Standard Skill Interfaces
- Base Skill Classes
- Manifest Validation
- AI Passport Validation
- Permission Enforcement
- Policy Evaluation
- Runtime Integration
- Verification Gates
- Audit Integration
- Registry Integration
- Testing Utilities
- Packaging Utilities
- Codex Automation Support

---

# SDK Scope

The SDK governs implementation for:

- Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills
- Skill Manifests
- Skill Policies
- Permission Profiles
- Runtime Profiles
- Verification Evidence
- Registry Records
- Lifecycle Transitions
- Skill Packages
- Skill Test Suites

---

# SDK Modules

The target SDK contains:

```text
sdk/
├── core/
├── contracts/
├── identity/
├── permissions/
├── policies/
├── runtime/
├── registry/
├── verification/
├── audit/
├── lifecycle/
├── packaging/
├── testing/
└── exceptions/
```

---

# Core Module

The Core module provides:

- BaseEnterpriseSkill
- SkillContext
- SkillInput
- SkillOutput
- SkillResult
- SkillMetadata
- SkillStatus
- SkillLifecycleState

No production Skill may bypass the Core module.

---

# Contracts Module

The Contracts module defines stable interfaces for:

- Skill Execution
- Skill Validation
- Skill Initialization
- Skill Shutdown
- Policy Evaluation
- Permission Evaluation
- Runtime Allocation
- Audit Recording
- Registry Synchronization
- Verification Evidence

Contracts must remain versioned and backward compatible where possible.

---

# Identity Module

The Identity module integrates:

- AI Passport
- Skill Identity
- Agent Identity
- Runtime Identity
- Workload Identity
- Digital Signatures

Execution without verified identity is prohibited.

---

# Permission Module

The Permission module enforces:

- Least Privilege
- Deny By Default
- Capability Permissions
- Runtime Permissions
- Data Permissions
- Network Permissions
- External Service Permissions

Undeclared permissions are denied.

---

# Policy Module

The Policy module evaluates:

- Architecture Policies
- Security Policies
- Runtime Policies
- Data Policies
- Network Policies
- Lifecycle Policies
- Approval Policies
- Public/Private Boundary Policies

Policy failure blocks execution.

---

# Runtime Module

The Runtime module provides:

- Runtime Initialization
- Sandbox Binding
- Resource Limits
- Timeout Enforcement
- Retry Policies
- Execution Isolation
- Runtime Monitoring
- Controlled Shutdown

Every Skill executes inside an approved runtime profile.

---

# Registry Module

The Registry module provides:

- Skill Registration
- Skill Discovery
- Version Resolution
- Lifecycle Status
- Trust Tier Lookup
- Dependency Lookup
- Revocation Status
- Registry Synchronization

Unregistered Skills cannot enter production.

---

# Verification Module

The Verification module implements mandatory gates:

- G1 — Structure and Schema
- G2 — Security and Permissions
- G3 — Functional and Reliability Testing
- G4 — Approval Gateway Authorization

Every gate must produce evidence.

---

# Audit Module

The Audit module records:

- Skill Creation
- Skill Changes
- Validation Results
- Execution Events
- Permission Decisions
- Policy Decisions
- Approval Decisions
- Lifecycle Transitions
- Failures
- Revocations

Audit records must be tamper-evident.

---

# Lifecycle Module

The Lifecycle module governs:

- Draft
- Engineering
- Verification
- Approved
- Production
- Suspended
- Deprecated
- Revoked
- Archived

Invalid transitions are prohibited.

---

# Packaging Module

The Packaging module creates governed Skill packages containing:

```text
skill-name/
├── SKILL.md
├── skill.manifest.yaml
├── policy.yaml
├── permissions.yaml
├── runtime.yaml
├── src/
├── tests/
└── evidence/
```

Packages must be reproducible and digitally signed before production release.

---

# Testing Module

The Testing module provides:

- Unit Test Base
- Integration Test Base
- Security Test Base
- Policy Test Base
- Permission Test Base
- Runtime Test Base
- Adversarial Test Base
- Verification Evidence Helpers

A Skill without tests cannot be production-approved.

---

# Exception Model

The SDK must define explicit exceptions for:

- InvalidManifestError
- IdentityValidationError
- PermissionDeniedError
- PolicyDeniedError
- RuntimeValidationError
- VerificationFailedError
- ApprovalRequiredError
- SkillRevokedError
- RegistrySynchronizationError
- AuditWriteError

Undefined failure behaviour is prohibited.

---

# Skill Execution Contract

Every Skill execution follows:

Request

↓

Manifest Validation

↓

Identity Validation

↓

AI Passport Validation

↓

Permission Validation

↓

Policy Evaluation

↓

Runtime Allocation

↓

Skill Execution

↓

Output Validation

↓

Audit Recording

↓

Result Return

---

# Base Skill Requirements

Every implementation derived from the SDK must define:

- Skill ID
- Canonical Name
- Version
- Description
- Owner
- Responsible Team
- Input Contract
- Output Contract
- Required Permissions
- Runtime Profile
- Verification Requirements
- Lifecycle State

Incomplete Skills remain blocked from production.

---

# Codex Implementation Rules

Codex must:

- implement contracts before concrete Skills;
- preserve canonical terminology;
- generate typed interfaces;
- avoid hidden dependencies;
- avoid placeholder security controls;
- include tests with implementation;
- include evidence with verification;
- document assumptions;
- report incomplete work honestly;
- never claim production readiness without proof.

---

# Public / Private Boundary

The SDK belongs to the PRIVATE engineering boundary.

The following must not be published without explicit approval:

- internal contracts;
- security enforcement logic;
- permission architecture;
- runtime controls;
- verification logic;
- registry internals;
- audit implementation;
- production configurations.

---

# Repository Integration

The SDK must remain synchronized with:

- AGENTS.md
- CODEX.md
- ARCHITECTURE.md
- SECURITY.md
- GOVERNANCE.md
- REGISTRY.md
- MANIFEST.md
- VERIFICATION.md
- TRUST.md
- LIFECYCLE.md
- PERMISSIONS.md
- POLICIES.md
- AUDIT.md

---

# Target Engineering Sequence

Codex must implement the SDK in this order:

1. Core Types
2. Contracts
3. Exception Model
4. Manifest Validation
5. Identity Validation
6. Permission Enforcement
7. Policy Evaluation
8. Runtime Integration
9. Registry Integration
10. Verification Gates
11. Audit Integration
12. Lifecycle Enforcement
13. Packaging
14. Testing Utilities
15. First Reference Skill

---

# Production Readiness

The SDK is production-ready only when:

- all contracts are implemented;
- schemas are validated;
- tests pass;
- security controls are operational;
- verification evidence exists;
- audit integration works;
- registry synchronization works;
- lifecycle enforcement works;
- Approval Gateway integration is verified;
- Guardian Core controls are verified.

Documentation alone does not establish production readiness.

---

# Long-Term Vision

The Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills SDK becomes the canonical engineering foundation used by Codex and authorized engineering systems to build professional, governed, secure, reusable, and production-ready AI capabilities throughout IMPERIAL Core.

---

Author and Owner

Romaskevich Alexander Nikolaevich
# SDK_CONTRACTS.md

# 💎 Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills SDK Contracts

> Repository: Codex-Enterprise-IMPERIAL-Skills
> Visibility: 🔒 PRIVATE
> Project: IMPERIAL Core
> System: Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills (EIS)
> Status: Codex-Ready Engineering Specification

---

# Mission

The SDK Contracts define the canonical implementation interfaces for every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill.

Every Skill developed by Codex or engineering teams must implement these contracts.

Contracts guarantee compatibility, predictability, security, and long-term maintainability.

---

# Architecture Principles

- Contract First Engineering
- Architecture Before Implementation
- Interface Stability
- Zero Trust
- Explicit Contracts
- Complete Auditability

---

# Objectives

The SDK Contracts provide:

- Stable Interfaces
- Runtime Compatibility
- Version Compatibility
- Skill Standardization
- Secure Integration
- Enterprise Consistency

---

# Required Contracts

Every Enterprise Skill must implement:

- Initialization Contract
- Validation Contract
- Execution Contract
- Permission Contract
- Policy Contract
- Runtime Contract
- Audit Contract
- Shutdown Contract

Missing contracts are prohibited.

---

# Initialization Contract

Responsible for:

- loading configuration;
- validating manifest;
- initializing resources;
- registering runtime context.

Initialization failure blocks execution.

---

# Validation Contract

Responsible for validating:

- input schema;
- manifest;
- dependencies;
- runtime profile;
- AI Passport;
- Trust Tier.

Validation must complete before execution.

---

# Execution Contract

Responsible for:

- receiving input;
- executing business logic;
- producing output;
- reporting execution status.

Execution must remain deterministic.

---

# Permission Contract

Responsible for:

- permission verification;
- capability validation;
- resource authorization;
- external access validation.

Permission failures immediately terminate execution.

---

# Policy Contract

Responsible for:

- security policies;
- runtime policies;
- governance policies;
- enterprise compliance.

Policy violations block execution.

---

# Runtime Contract

Responsible for:

- runtime allocation;
- sandbox configuration;
- resource limits;
- timeout enforcement;
- cleanup.

Runtime isolation is mandatory.

---

# Audit Contract

Responsible for recording:

- initialization;
- execution;
- failures;
- approvals;
- policy decisions;
- lifecycle events.

Audit evidence must remain immutable.

---

# Shutdown Contract

Responsible for:

- releasing resources;
- clearing temporary state;
- final audit;
- graceful termination.

Resource leaks are prohibited.

---

# Contract Versioning

Every contract contains:

- Contract ID
- Version
- Compatibility Level
- Status
- Owner

Breaking changes require governance approval.

---

# Repository Synchronization

SDK Contracts remain synchronized with:

- SDK Architecture
- Runtime Engine
- Verification Framework
- Trust Framework
- Registry
- Audit Ledger

---

# Repository Classification

Repository Classification:

🔒 PRIVATE

SDK Contracts belong exclusively to the private engineering repository.

---

# Long-Term Vision

SDK Contracts establish the canonical implementation standard for Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills, enabling Codex, HANTER, Nano Core Agents, Legion, ORION, and future IMPERIAL Core systems to build interoperable, secure, deterministic, and enterprise-grade AI capabilities.

---

Author and Owner

Romaskevich Alexander Nikolaevich

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Reference Before Customization

- Manifest
- Metadata

Every Skill executes through:

Initialize

↓

Validate

↓

Authenticate

↓

Authorize

↓

Policy Evaluation

↓

Runtime Allocation

↓

Execute

↓

↓

Audit Recording

↓

Execution order is mandatory.

Every implementation includes:

- BaseEnterpriseSkill
- SDK Interfaces
- SDK Contracts
- Runtime Integration
- Registry Integration
- Verification Integration
- Audit Integration

---

# Security Requirements

Every implementation enforces:

- AI Passport Validation

- Audit Logging

```text
enterprise-skill/

├── SKILL.md

├── policies/
│
├── permissions/
│
├── runtime/

├── evidence/
│
├── docs/
│
└── artifacts/
```

---

# Ownership

Owner: Romaskevich Alexander Nikolaevich

Founder: Romaskevich Alexander Nikolaevich

Chief Systems Architect: Romaskevich Alexander Nikolaevich

---

# Mission

The SDK Validation Framework defines the canonical validation pipeline for every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill before it may execute, register, or enter production.

Validation protects the integrity of the IMPERIAL Core ecosystem.

No Skill is trusted without verification.

---

# Architecture Principles

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Validation Before Execution
- Zero Trust
- Evidence Before Trust
- Deterministic Validation
- Complete Auditability

---

# Objectives

The Validation Framework provides:

- Manifest Validation
- Schema Validation
- Dependency Validation
- Runtime Validation
- Permission Validation
- Policy Validation
- Security Validation
- Production Readiness Validation

---

# Validation Levels

Every Skill passes:

- Level 1 — Structure Validation
- Level 2 — Security Validation
- Level 3 — Runtime Validation
- Level 4 — Integration Validation
- Level 5 — Production Validation

Failure at any level blocks promotion.

---

# Validation Pipeline

Every validation follows:

Validation Request

↓

Manifest Validation

↓

SDK Contract Validation

↓

Dependency Validation

↓

AI Passport Validation

↓

Permission Validation

↓

Policy Validation

↓

Runtime Validation

↓

Security Validation

↓

Verification Evidence

↓

Audit Recording

↓

Validation Result

---

# Validation Categories

The framework validates:

- Project Structure
- Manifest Integrity
- SDK Compliance
- Runtime Compatibility
- Dependencies
- Permissions
- Policies
- Security Controls
- Test Coverage
- Documentation

---

# Validation Results

Possible outcomes:

- PASSED
- PASSED WITH WARNINGS
- FAILED
- BLOCKED

Only **PASSED** qualifies a Skill for production.

---

# Automatic Validation

Codex automatically validates:

- every generated Skill;
- every repository change;
- every release candidate;
- every production package.

Validation cannot be skipped.

---

# Repository Synchronization

The Validation Framework remains synchronized with:

- SDK Architecture

- Verification Exceptions

- InvalidManifestException
- SchemaValidationException
- RuntimeInitializationException
- PermissionDeniedException
- PolicyViolationException

- RegistryAccessException
- AuditRecordingException

- FATAL

---

# Mission

The SDK Skill Certification Framework governs how Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills are independently evaluated, certified, approved, renewed, suspended, and revoked before entering or remaining in production.

Certification confirms that a Skill satisfies enterprise engineering, security, governance, quality, and operational standards.

Certification is mandatory for every production Skill.

---

# Architecture Principles

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Certification Before Production
- Zero Trust
- Independent Verification
- Continuous Compliance
- Complete Auditability

---

# Objectives

The Certification Framework provides:

- Skill Certification
- Engineering Assessment
- Security Assessment
- Operational Assessment
- Certification Renewal
- Certification Revocation

---

# Certification Levels

Supported certification levels include:

- Development
- Engineering Verified
- Enterprise Certified
- Production Certified
- Mission Critical Certified
- Sovereign Certified

Production deployment requires at least **Production Certified** status.

---

# Certification Pipeline

Every certification follows:

Certification Request

↓

Architecture Review

↓

SDK Validation

↓

Testing

↓

Security Assessment

↓

Performance Assessment

↓

Verification

↓

Approval Gateway

↓

Audit Recording

↓

Certification Issued

---

# Certification Requirements

Every Skill must satisfy:

- SDK Compliance
- Manifest Compliance
- Runtime Compatibility
- Security Validation
- Policy Compliance
- Permission Validation
- Verification Evidence
- Complete Test Coverage
- Audit Registration

Failure blocks certification.

---

# Certification Renewal

Certified Skills are periodically re-evaluated after:

- major version changes;
- security updates;
- dependency changes;
- policy changes;
- runtime changes;
- governance updates.

Certification remains continuously governed.

---

# Revocation Rules

Certification is revoked when:

- critical security failures occur;
- verification evidence becomes invalid;
- policy violations are detected;
- trust level falls below the minimum threshold;
- audit integrity cannot be verified.

Revoked Skills cannot execute.

- Certification Status

Promotion without evidence is prohibited.

---

# Deprecation Rules

A Skill may be deprecated when:

- a newer version replaces it;
- security risks are identified;
- dependencies become unsupported;
- architecture evolves;
- governance requires replacement.

Deprecated Skills remain traceable.

---

# Retirement Rules

A retired Skill:

- cannot execute;
- cannot receive updates;
- remains available for audit;
- preserves historical evidence;
- remains linked to dependent records.

Retirement never removes audit history.

---

# Repository Synchronization

The Lifecycle Framework remains synchronized with:

- SDK Architecture
- SDK Validation Framework
- SDK Testing Framework
- SDK Skill Certification Framework
- Runtime Engine
- Registry
- Verification Framework
- Guardian Core
- Approval Gateway
- Audit Ledger

---

# Repository Classification

Repository Classification:

🔒 PRIVATE

SDK Skill Lifecycle Management belongs exclusively to the private engineering repository.

---

The Runtime Engine provides deterministic execution, enterprise governance, security enforcement, runtime isolation and operational stability for every Enterprise IMPERIAL Skill.

No Enterprise Skill may execute outside an approved Runtime Engine.

---

# Architecture Principles

- Architecture Before Implementation
- Federated Multi-Orchestrator Architecture
- Zero Trust
- Runtime Isolation
- Enterprise Governance
- Deterministic Execution
- Continuous Verification
- Complete Auditability

---

# Primary Responsibilities

The Runtime Engine is responsible for:

- Skill Execution
- Runtime Isolation
- Resource Management
- Security Enforcement
- Execution Verification
- Runtime Monitoring
- Failure Recovery
- Enterprise Audit Support

---

# Runtime Components

The Runtime Engine consists of:

- Execution Controller
- Skill Loader
- Runtime Sandbox
- Security Manager
- Resource Manager
- Policy Validator
- Health Monitor
- Runtime Monitor
- Execution Verifier
- Audit Connector

---

# Runtime Execution Pipeline

Every execution request follows the canonical sequence:

Execution Request

↓

Skill Discovery

↓

Skill Verification

↓

AI Passport Validation

↓

Approval Gateway Authorization

↓

Guardian Core Policy Validation

↓

Runtime Allocation

↓

Sandbox Initialization

↓

Skill Loading

↓

Execution

↓

Continuous Monitoring

↓

Output Verification

↓

Audit Recording

↓

Execution Completed

---

# Runtime Validation

Before execution the Runtime Engine validates:

- Runtime Health
- Runtime Capacity
- Runtime Availability
- Skill Certification
- Trust Tier
- Policy Compliance
- Permission Scope
- Resource Availability

Any validation failure immediately terminates the execution request.

---

# Runtime Isolation

Every execution environment provides:

- Process Isolation
- Memory Isolation
- Filesystem Isolation
- Network Isolation
- Secret Isolation
- Session Isolation
- Resource Isolation

Isolation guarantees that Enterprise Skills cannot interfere with one another.

---

# Runtime Monitoring

Continuous monitoring includes:

- CPU Usage
- Memory Usage
- Storage Usage
- Network Activity
- Runtime Health
- Execution Duration
- Policy Compliance
- Security Events
- Performance Metrics

All operational events are continuously monitored and recorded.

---

# Runtime Security

Security mechanisms include:

- Zero Trust Enforcement
- Identity Verification
- Runtime Authentication
- Policy Enforcement
- Secure Communication
- Runtime Integrity Validation
- Continuous Threat Monitoring

Security controls remain active throughout the execution lifecycle.

---

# Runtime Recovery

Recovery capabilities include:

- Automatic Retry
- Runtime Restart
- Session Recovery
- Resource Reallocation
- Runtime Failover
- Execution Continuation

Recovery preserves execution integrity whenever technically possible.

---

# Governance Rules

Every Runtime Engine must:

- remain registered;
- remain certified;
- remain continuously monitored;
- enforce Enterprise policies;
- support verification;
- preserve audit evidence;
- report operational health.

Runtime Engines violating Enterprise Governance are automatically isolated.

---

# Repository Synchronization

The Runtime Engine remains synchronized with:

- SDK Skill Runtime Coordinator
- SDK Skill Execution Engine
- SDK Runtime Client
- Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills Registry
- Runtime Registry
- Guardian Core
- Approval Gateway
- Audit Ledger
- Verification Framework

---

# Repository Classification

Repository Classification:

🔒 PRIVATE

SDK Runtime Engine belongs exclusively to the private engineering repository.

---

# Long-Term Vision

The SDK Runtime Engine establishes the canonical execution platform for Enterprise IMPERIAL Skills, enabling Codex to execute secure, scalable, resilient and enterprise-grade Runtime workloads across Federated Runtime Domains while preserving governance, auditability, operational integrity and the canonical architecture of IMPERIAL Core.

---

No Runtime Session may exist outside Enterprise Governance.
